\widetilde{T}C\coloneqq\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-18.18416pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hskip-0.73747pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.13747pt\hskip 18.18416pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-17.88904pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-0.66248pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-5.79582pt\hskip 17.88904pt}}\left[Jb,C\right]\times Tb.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{T}C
be the oplax coend
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-18.18416pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hskip-0.73747pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.13747pt\hskip 18.18416pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-17.88904pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-0.66248pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-5.79582pt\hskip 17.88904pt}}\left[Jb,C\right]\times Tb
, in which case the assignment
C\mapsto\widetilde{T}C
uniquely lifts to a 2-functor
\widetilde{T}\colon{\bf CAT}\to{\bf CAT}
by the universal properties of oplax coends. For each pair of categories
C
and
D
we then have the following chain of natural isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\left[\widetilde{T}C,D\right]

\displaystyle\cong\left[\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-18.18416pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hskip-0.73747pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.13747pt\hskip 18.18416pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int^{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-17.88904pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-0.66248pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-5.79582pt\hskip 17.88904pt}}\left[Jb,C\right]\times Tb,D\right]

\displaystyle\cong\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-18.18416pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hskip-0.73747pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.13747pt\hskip 18.18416pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-17.88904pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-0.66248pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-5.79582pt\hskip 17.88904pt}}\big[\left[Jb,C\right]\times Tb,D\big]

(
[
Hir22
, §3.2]
)

\displaystyle\cong\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-20.82918pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\hskip-0.89996pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.87776pt\hskip 20.82918pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-18.18416pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hskip-0.73747pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-6.13747pt\hskip 18.18416pt}}{\hbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int_{b\in\mathcal{B}}$}\hskip-17.88904pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-0.66248pt\rightarrow$}}\hskip-5.79582pt\hskip 17.88904pt}}\big[\left[Jb,C\right],\left[Tb,D\right]\big]

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{Lax}\left[{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\bf CAT}\right]\big(\left[J-,C\right],\left[T-,D\right]\big),

(
[
Hir22
, §3.1]
)

thus exhibiting
\widetilde{T}
as the left oplax Kan extension of
T
along
J
.
∎

We conclude this appendix by finishing the proof of Proposition
3.10
.

Proposition A.6
.

For each category
C
, the category
\widetilde{T}C
of Definition
3.7
is the oplax coend of the 2-functor

H_{C}\colon{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\mathcal{B}\to{\bf CAT}\qquad H_{C}(b,b^{\prime})\coloneqq\left[Jb,C\right]\times Tb^{\prime}

Proof.

First, we show that
\widetilde{T}C
is the vertex of an oplax cowedge
\lambda
on
H_{C}
.

(1)

For each
b\in\mathcal{B}
, let
\lambda_{b}\colon H_{C}(b,b)\to\widetilde{T}C
be the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h\colon Jb\to C,\nu\in Tb)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(b,h\colon Jb\to C,\nu\in Tb)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{\prime}\colon Jb\to C,\nu^{\prime}\in Tb)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(b,h^{\prime}\colon Jb\to C,\nu^{\prime}\in Tb).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha,\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{id},\alpha,\varphi)}

(2)

For each 1-cell
f\colon b^{\prime}\to b\in\mathcal{B}
, let
\lambda_{f}\colon\lambda_{b}\circ H_{C}(\operatorname{id},f)\Rightarrow\lambda_{b^{\prime}}\circ H_{C}(f,\operatorname{id})
be the natural transformation defined at an object
(h\colon Jb\to C,\nu^{\prime}\in Tb^{\prime})
by the arrow

(f,\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})\colon(b,h,Tf(\nu^{\prime}))\to(b^{\prime},h\circ Jf,\nu^{\prime}).

To show that this assignment defines an oplax cowedge
\lambda\colon H_{C}\to\widetilde{T}C
, we need to verify that it satisfies the three axioms of Definition
A.1
. Axiom (a) and (b) are immediate, so we only show axiom (c), i.e. that for
f,g\colon b^{\prime}\to b
and
\sigma\colon f\Rightarrow g
in
\mathcal{B}
, the pastings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b,b^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b,b),}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(g,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(\operatorname{id},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(\operatorname{id},g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(\operatorname{id},\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{g}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b,b^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{C}(b,b)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(g,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(f,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(\operatorname{id},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{C}(\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}

coincide. Given
(h\colon Jb\to C,\nu^{\prime}\in Tb^{\prime})\in H_{C}(b,b^{\prime})
, the component at
(h,\nu^{\prime})
of the first pasting is represented by the triple
(g,\operatorname{id},(T\sigma)_{\nu^{\prime}})
, while that of the second pasting is represented by the triple
(f,h*J\sigma,\operatorname{id})
. Evidently,
\sigma\colon f\Rightarrow g
witnesses the equivalence of these two arrows as in Definition
3.7
, so the two components coincide.

Finally, we show that
\lambda
exhibits
\widetilde{T}C
as the oplax coend of
H_{C}
. Let
\gamma\colon H_{C}\to A
be another oplax cowedge. The unique functor
u\colon\widetilde{T}C\to A
such that
\gamma=u\lambda
is evidently the functor defined:

(1)

on objects by mapping
(b,h\colon Jb\to C,\nu\in Tb)\in\widetilde{T}C
to
\gamma_{b}(h,\nu)\in A
;

(2)

on arrows by mapping
(f,\alpha,\varphi)\colon(b,h,\nu)\to(b^{\prime},h^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})\in\widetilde{T}C
to the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma_{b}(h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma_{b}(h,Tf(\nu^{\prime}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma_{b^{\prime}}(h\circ Jf,\nu^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma_{b^{\prime}}(h^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{b}(\operatorname{id},\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma_{f})_{(h,\nu^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{b^{\prime}}(\alpha,\operatorname{id})}

Let
u,v\colon\widetilde{T}C\to A
be functors and let
\Psi\colon u\lambda\Rightarrow v\lambda
be a modification of oplax cowedges. The unique natural transformation
\psi\colon u\Rightarrow v
such that
\Psi=\psi*\lambda
is evidently the natural transformation defined at an object
(b,h\colon Jb\to C,\nu\in Tb)\in\widetilde{T}C
by the arrow
(\Psi_{b})_{(h,\nu)}\colon u(b,h,\nu)\to v(b,h,\nu)
.
∎

Appendix B
Omitted proofs from Section
5

In this appendix we give some of the routine proofs omitted from Section
5
.

Lemma B.1
.

Given a colax algebra
\braket{C,(-)^{C},\Gamma,\Delta}
for the
J
-relative 2-monad
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
, the tuple
\braket{C,\Phi,\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{m}}
defined in
5.1.1
is a colax algebra for the pseudomonad
\braket{\widetilde{T},\eta^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp}}
.

Proof.

We will verify (half of) axiom (a) of Definition
5.1
, i.e. that the composite natural transformation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}^{2}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta^{\sharp}_{\widetilde{T}C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mu^{\sharp}_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta^{\sharp}_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{p}_{C}^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{i}}}

coincides with the identity on
\Phi
. Fix an object
(b,h\colon Jb\to C,\nu)\in\widetilde{T}C
. Note that, by definition,
\eta^{\sharp}_{\widetilde{T}C}(b,h,\nu)=(1_{\mathcal{B}},(b,h,\nu)\colon 1_{{\bf CAT}}\to\widetilde{T}C,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))
. Hence, recalling the definition of
\mu^{\sharp}
, we have that
\mu^{\sharp}_{C}(1_{\mathcal{B}},(b,h,\nu),\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))
is given by the triple
(\ell,a\colon J\ell\to C,Q^{*}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet)))
, where
\ell\in\mathcal{B}
is the oplax colimit of the constant diagram
{(Jb)}^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{B}
of value
1_{\mathcal{B}}
,
a\colon J\ell\to C
is uniquely induced as
C
is an oplax cocone on the same diagram, and
Q\colon 1_{{\bf CAT}}\to T\ell
is such that
Q(\bullet)=Q^{*}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}(\bullet)})=Tu(\nu)
, where
u\colon b\to\ell
is the invertible arrow constructed in Proposition
4.9
. Denote with
\bar{u}\colon\ell\to b
its inverse, so that
h\circ J\bar{u}=a
and
T\bar{u}(Q(\bullet))=\nu
.

With this notation, we then need to show that the composite:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\mu^{\sharp}_{C}\eta^{\sharp}_{\widetilde{T}C})(b,h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi\eta^{\sharp}_{\widetilde{T}C})(b,h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\eta^{\sharp}_{C}\Phi)(b,h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi*\mathfrak{p}_{C}^{-1})_{(b,h,\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{m}*\eta^{\sharp}_{\widetilde{T}C})_{(b,h,\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{i}*\Phi)_{(b,h,\nu)}}

is the identity, which is also equal to the composite:

(*)\hbox to401.15pt{\vbox to21.57pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 200.57172pt\lower-9.11586pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Unwapping definitions,
(*)
coincides with the composite:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}(\nu)=}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}T\bar{u}(Q(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u})^{C}(Q(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(hJ\bar{u})^{C}(Q(\bullet))=}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q(\bullet)=}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q^{*}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(a^{C}Q)^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\circ(b,h,\nu))^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))=}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\nu)^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}(\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta_{h,\eta_{b}J\bar{u}}*Q)_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u})^{C}*Q)_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta_{a,Q}*\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}})_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau^{C}*\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}})_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma_{h^{C}\nu})_{\bullet}}

Note then that, by naturality of the family
\set{\Gamma}
with respect to
\tau
and by axiom (b) of a colax algebra for
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
(see Definition
1.4
), the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q^{*}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q(\bullet)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(a^{C}Q)^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q(\bullet)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\circ(b,h,\nu))^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\nu)^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}(\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Delta_{a,Q}*\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}})_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{a^{C}Q})_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\tau^{C}*\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}})_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{h^{C}\nu})_{\bullet}}}

commutes, meaning that we can reduce to showing that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}T\bar{u}(Q(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u})^{C}(Q(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(hJ\bar{u})^{C}(Q(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}Q(\bullet)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Delta_{h,\eta_{b}J\bar{u}}*Q)_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u})^{C}*Q)_{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\bullet}}

commutes. Expanding, this is the outer border of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}T\bar{u}(Tu(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(aJu)^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\eta_{b})^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u})^{C}\eta_{\ell}Ju)^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(a^{C}\eta_{\ell}Ju)^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u})^{C}(Tu(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((hJ\bar{u})^{C}\eta_{\ell}Ju)^{C}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(hJ\bar{u})^{C}(Tu(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a^{C}(Tu(\nu))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\Delta_{h,\eta_{b}J\bar{u}}*Tu)_{\nu}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{h})^{C}_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u}}*Ju)^{C}_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u})^{C}*\eta_{\ell}Ju)^{C}_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{a}*Ju)^{C}_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Delta_{h^{C}\eta_{b}J\bar{u},\eta_{\ell}Ju})_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{((\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u})^{C}*Tu)_{\nu}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Gamma_{hJ\bar{u}}*Ju)^{C}_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Delta_{a,\eta Ju})_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(i)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(ii)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(iv)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(iii)}}

where:

(i)

commutes by axioms (a), (b) and (c) of a colax algebra for
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
;

(ii)

commutes by naturality of the family
\set{\Gamma}
with respect to
\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u}
;

(iii)

commutes being
h\circ J\bar{u}=a
;

(iv)

commutes by naturality of the family
\set{\Delta}
with respect to
\Gamma_{h}*J\bar{u}
.

The other axioms can be shown with similar arguments.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Given a colax morphism
\braket{U,\upsilon}\colon\braket{C,(-)^{C},\Gamma,\Delta}\to\braket{C^{\prime},(-)^{C^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}
of colax algebras for the
J
-relative 2-monad
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
, the pair
\braket{U,\mathfrak{u}}
defined in
5.1.2
is a colax morphism
\braket{C,\Phi,\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{m}}\to\braket{C^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\mathfrak{i}^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}
of colax algebras for the pseudomonad
\braket{\widetilde{T},\eta^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp}}
.

Proof.

We will verify axiom (a) of Definition
5.1
, that is the following pastings coincide:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{u}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta_{C}^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta_{C^{\prime}}^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta_{C}^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{i}}}

Fix an object
c\in C
. Showing that the above equality holds amounts to showing that the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(U\circ\Phi\circ\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(c)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\widetilde{T}U\circ\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(c)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U(c)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\eta^{\sharp}_{C^{\prime}}\circ U)(c)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{u}*\eta^{\sharp}_{C})_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U*\mathfrak{i})_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}*U)_{c}}

commutes. Unwrapping the definitions, this coincides with the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U(c^{C}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet)))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Uc)^{C^{\prime}}(\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U(c)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\upsilon_{c})_{\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U((\Gamma_{c})_{\bullet})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma^{\prime}_{Uc})_{\bullet}}

which commutes by axiom (a) of a colax morphism of colax algebras for
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
. Axiom (b) can be shown similarly.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Given a colax algebra
\braket{C,\Phi,\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{m}}
for the pseudomonad
\braket{\widetilde{T},\eta^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp}}
, the tuple
\braket{C,(-)^{C},\Gamma,\Delta}
defined in
5.2.1
is a colax algebra for the
J
-relative 2-monad
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
.

Proof.

We will verify axiom (a) of Definition
1.4
, i.e. that for each functor
h\colon Jb\to C
, the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}\eta^{*}_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(h^{C}\eta_{b})^{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h^{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{h,\eta_{b}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma_{h})^{C}}

commutes. Let
\nu\in Tb
. Unwrapping the definitions of
\Delta
and of
(-)^{C}
, this means showing that the composite:

(*)\hbox to387.03pt{\vbox to74.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 193.51436pt\lower-35.93027pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {}{

{{{{{}}{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{103.51572pt}{-23.16777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

is the identity, where
(\ell,a\colon J\ell\to C,(b,Q\colon Jb\to T\ell,\nu))\coloneqq\mathfrak{s}_{T,T}(b,\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b},\nu)
as in
5.2.1
and where
t\colon\ell\to b\in\mathcal{B}
is the canonical 1-cell induced being
b
an oplax cocone on the diagram of which
\ell
is the oplax colimit. Consider then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(\ell,a,Q^{*}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\circ\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(b,\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b},\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\circ\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(b,\eta^{\sharp}_{C}h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(\bar{\ell},\bar{a},\bar{Q}^{*}\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu)),}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(t,\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi\circ\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(\operatorname{id},\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(\bar{v},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}

where the arrow

(\bar{v},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})\colon(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))\to(\bar{\ell},\bar{a},\bar{Q}^{*}\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))\coloneqq(\mu^{\sharp}_{C}\circ\widetilde{T}\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))

is the component at
(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))
of the right unit
\mathfrak{q}_{C}
, meaning that
\bar{v}\colon\bar{\ell}\to b
is the inverse of the canonical invertible 1-cell
v\colon b\to\bar{\ell}
defined in Proposition
4.9
, and where
\sigma\colon\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b}\Rightarrow\eta_{C}^{\sharp}h
is the natural transformation defined on
x\in Jb
by the arrow

(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})\colon\iota_{b}(h)(\eta_{b}(x))=(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))\to(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))=\eta_{C}^{\sharp}(h(x)).

Note that, by the universal properties of oplax colimits (and by functoriality of
\Phi
), the above diagram commutes. Moreover, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(b,\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b},\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi)(b,\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b},\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h^{C}\eta_{b},\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(b,\eta^{\sharp}_{C}h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi)(b,\eta^{\sharp}_{C}h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi\widetilde{T}\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{(b,\iota_{b}(h)\eta_{b},\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(\operatorname{id},\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi)(\operatorname{id},\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(\operatorname{id},\Phi*\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{(b,\eta_{C}^{\sharp}h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))}}

clearly commutes, by naturality of
\mathfrak{m}
(for the left-hand square) and by definition of
\widetilde{T}\Phi
(for the right-hand square). Finally, note that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h^{C}\eta_{b},\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\circ\widetilde{T}\Phi\circ\widetilde{T}\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(\operatorname{id},\Gamma_{h},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(\operatorname{id},\Phi*\sigma,\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi((\widetilde{T}\mathfrak{i})_{(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))})}

also commutes. This follows by functoriality of
\Phi
as the diagram of natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi\circ\iota_{b}(h)\circ\eta_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi\circ\eta_{C}^{\sharp}\circ h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi*\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}*h}

commutes, i.e. the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma_{h})_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}_{h(x)}}

commutes for each
x\in Jb
, which is exactly the definition of
(\Gamma_{h})_{x}
. Summing up, pasting commutative squares, we have shown that
(*)
is the identity if and only if the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(\ell^{\prime},a^{\prime},Q^{\prime*}\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{=(\Phi\mu^{\sharp}_{C})(b,\eta^{\sharp}_{C}h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi)(b,\eta^{\sharp}_{C}h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{=(\Phi\widetilde{T}\Phi\widetilde{T}\eta^{\sharp}_{C})(b,h,\eta^{*}_{b}(\nu))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(b,h,\nu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi(v,\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{m}_{(b,\eta_{C}^{\sharp}h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi((\widetilde{T}\mathfrak{i})_{(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))})}}

is also the identity. To conclude, note that this latter composite is the component at
(b,h,\eta_{b}^{*}(\nu))
of the composite natural transformation represented in the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}^{2}C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\mathfrak{m}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\widetilde{T}\eta^{\sharp}_{C}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\mu^{\sharp}_{C}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\widetilde{T}\Phi}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\mathfrak{q}_{C}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\widetilde{T}\mathfrak{i}}}}}

which, by axiom (a) of a colax algebra in Definition
5.1
, is indeed the identity natural transformation. The other axioms can be shown with similar arguments.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

Given a colax morphism
\braket{U,\mathfrak{u}}\colon\braket{C,\Phi,\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{m}}\to\braket{C^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\mathfrak{i}^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}
of colax algebras for the pseudomonad
\braket{\widetilde{T},\eta^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp}}
, the pair
\braket{U,\upsilon}
defined in
5.2.2
is a colax morphism
\braket{C,(-)^{C},\Gamma,\Delta}\to\braket{C^{\prime},(-)^{C^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}
of colax algebras for the
J
-relative 2-monad
\braket{T,\eta,(-)^{*}}
.

Proof.

We will verify axiom (a) of Definition
1.4
, i.e. that for each functor
h\colon Jb\to C
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Uh^{C}\eta_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Uh)^{C^{\prime}}\eta_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Uh}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{h}*\eta_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U*\Gamma_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{\prime}_{Uh}}

commutes. Let
x\in Jb
. Explicitly, this means showing that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\Phi(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\widetilde{T}U)(b,Uh,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi^{\prime}(b,Uh,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\Phi(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi^{\prime}(1_{\mathcal{B}},Uhx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Uh(x)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}_{(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\Phi(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\prime}(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(\mathfrak{i}_{h(x)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}_{Uh(x)}}

commutes. This follows by noting that this diagram factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\Phi(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\widetilde{T}U)(b,Uh,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi^{\prime}(b,Uh,\eta_{b}(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\Phi(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\widetilde{T}U)(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi^{\prime}(1_{\mathcal{B}},Uhx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Uh(x)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}_{(b,h,\eta_{b}(x))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\Phi(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\widetilde{T}U)(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\prime}(\dot{x},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{u}_{(1_{\mathcal{B}},hx,\eta_{1_{\mathcal{B}}}(\bullet))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(\mathfrak{i}_{h(x)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(iii)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}_{Uh(x)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ii)}

where (i) commutes by naturality of
\mathfrak{u}
, (ii) commutes by definition of
\widetilde{T}U
, and (iii) commutes by axiom (a) of a colax morphism in Definition
5.1
. Axiom (b) can be shown similarly.
∎
